# Israel at the FIFA World Cup

&#x20;This is a **record of the [Israel national team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_national_football_team)'s results at the [FIFA World Cup](11370.md)**. They have qualified for the tournament on one occasion, in [1970](157227.md).[^cite_note-Stat_Kit-1] Israel qualified for the 1970 World Cup as an Asian team. Nowadays, Israel competes in the European zone, as well as being a full member of [UEFA](32332.md) since the 1990s.

## History

Both in 1934 and in 1938, [Mandatory Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandatory_Palestine_national_football_team) competed in the FIFA World Cup qualifiers.[^cite_note-fifaprel-2][^cite_note-fifapal-3] In 1970, the [Israel national football team](9282173.md) competed in the [FIFA World Cup](11370.md) final-tournament for the very first time. [FIFA](11049.md) states in reference to this 1930s Palestine Mandate team that Asia's ([AFC](715249.md)'s) 'Palestine team', which had participated in previous competitions during the 1930s, was actually the forerunner of current Europe's ([UEFA](32332.md)'s) [Israel](9282173.md) national team; and, as such, bears no-relation to the national team of the [Palestinian Authority](24093.md) whatsoever.[^cite_note-fifaprel-2]

## 1970 FIFA World Cup

At the [1970 FIFA World Cup](157227.md), Israel participated for the first time. Israel qualified for the FIFA World Cup for the first time in 1970, along with [El Salvador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Salvador_national_football_team) and [Morocco](1024865.md).[^cite_note-Stat_Kit-1]

Although it was reported in the build-up to the final draw that seedings would be used, as had been the case at the previous two World Cup Finals,[^cite_note-4][^cite_note-5] the FIFA Organising Committee ultimately announced that there would be no seeding of teams.[^cite_note-FIFA_seeding-6] Instead, the sixteen teams were divided into four 'geographical groupings', which also took into account the teams' strengths and even political considerations;[^cite_note-7] the system ensured that Israel and Morocco would not be drawn to face each other after Morocco had earlier threatened to withdraw from the tournament, as they had done from the Olympic football tournament two years earlier,[^cite_note-8] if that were the case.[^cite_note-9]

### 1970 Qualifying Round

Main article: [1970 FIFA World Cup qualification (AFC and OFC)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1970_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_\(AFC_and_OFC\))

#### 1970 Qualifying Round 1

During the 1970 FIFA World Cup qualification Israel received a [First round](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1970_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_AFC_and_OFC_first_round?action=edit\&redlink=1) bye in the first round and moved directly to the [Second round](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1970_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_AFC_and_OFC_second_round?action=edit\&redlink=1).

#### 1970 Qualifying Round 2

In the second round, [North Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Korea_national_football_team), quarter-finalists at the previous tournament, were disqualified after refusing to play in Israel for political reasons.[^cite_note-10][^cite_note-Glanville-11]&#x20;

## Qualifier history

Through the 2026 qualifier, Israel has entered the qualifiers for the World Cup on 23 occasions. In 1934 and 1938, Palestine, under the British Mandate, competed. As Israel was established in place of Palestine in 1948, Israel began competing in 1950. In all years but 1970 Israel failed to qualify for the World Cup.

1934 FIFA World Cup qualifier

At the [1934 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1934_FIFA_World_Cup), [Mandatory Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandatory_Palestine_national_football_team) competed in the [Africa and Asia Group 12](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1934_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification#Group_12) qualifying round.[^cite_note-13] Mandatory Palestine finished in second place and was eliminated.

The Palestine football team consisted of nine British footballers, six Jewish footballers and one Arab footballer.[^cite_note-14] FIFA states in reference to the 1930s Palestine Mandate team that the 'Palestine team' that had participated in previous competitions in the 1930s, was actually the forerunner of today's Israel team and as such bears no relation to the national team of the Palestinian authority.[^cite_note-fifaprel-2] However, the region currently known as Palestine is considered "one of the first Asian teams to compete in the FIFA World Cup qualifiers".[^cite_note-fifapal-3]

1938 FIFA World Cup qualifier

At the [1938 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1938_FIFA_World_Cup), [Mandatory Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandatory_Palestine_national_football_team) competed in the [UEFA Group 6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1938_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_Group_6) qualifying round.[^cite_note-15] Hungary, as the strongest team of this group, was seeded. Greece and Mandatory Palestine would play against each other on a home-and-away basis, with Hungary playing against the winner at home. Mandatory Palestine lost to Greece in the First Round, and finished in third and last place.

In 1938, World Cup marked the second ([1934](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandatory_Palestine_at_the_1934_FIFA_World_Cup?action=edit\&redlink=1) being the first) and final time Mandatory Palestine competed in the World Cup. FIFA states in reference to the 1930s Palestine Mandate team that the 'Palestine team' that had participated in previous competitions in the 1930s was actually the forerunner of today's Israel team, and as such bears no relation to the national team of the Palestinian authority.[^cite_note-fifaprel-2]

1950 FIFA World Cup qualifier

At the [1950 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1950_FIFA_World_Cup), Israel competed in the [UEFA Group 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1950_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification#Group_3) qualifying round. Israel finished in third and last place.[^cite_note-16]

This World Cup was the first for Israel, although they previously competed in 1934 and 1938 as Mandatory Palestine. FIFA states in reference to the 1930s Palestine Mandate team that the 'Palestine team' that had participated in previous competitions in the 1930s was actually the forerunner of today's Israel team and as such bears no relation to the national team of the Palestinian authority.[^cite_note-fifaprel-2]

| Pos | Team | Pld | W | D | L | GF | GA | GD | Pts |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | [Yugoslavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yugoslavia_national_football_team) | 2 | 2 | 0 | 0 | 11 | 2 | +9 | 4 |
| 2 | [Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_national_football_team) | 2 | 0 | 0 | 2 | 2 | 11 | −9 | 0 |

Source:&#x20;

*Source:*[^cite_note-17]

Matches

Israel competed in Group 3, which had 3 teams each. The strongest team, France, was seeded. The winner of the First Round would move on to the Final Round. Israel lost to Yugoslavia in the First Round and was eliminated.

1958 FIFA World Cup qualifier

At the [1958 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1958_FIFA_World_Cup), Israel competed in the [Africa and Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1958_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification#Africa_and_Asia) qualification round. The round was conducted in a knockout stage format.[^cite_note-19] Israel won its group by default because its three opponents, [Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkey_national_football_team), [Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesia_national_football_team) and [Sudan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sudan_national_football_team), refused to play.

The national team was placed in the African/Asian zone and was drawn to play against Turkey in the first round. However, Turkey withdrew in protest of being placed in the African/Asian zone (instead of the European Zone), and Israel advanced to the second round without playing a match, along with Indonesia, Egypt and Sudan. Israel was drawn to play Indonesia, but, as Indonesia refused to play in Israel and as FIFA rejected their request to play against Israel on neutral ground, Indonesia withdrew and Israel advanced to the regional finals, again without playing a match, alongside Sudan. In the final round, Sudan refused to play Israel for political reasons and withdrew. FIFA had imposed a rule that no team would qualify without playing at least one match, after it had happened in several previous World Cups. Wales, which finished second in its group behind [Czechoslovakia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czechoslovakia_national_football_team), was drawn into a play-off, which they won.

CAF / AFC Preliminary Round - Group 2

Turkey refused to compete in the Asian group, so Israel advanced to the Second Round automatically.

| Rank | Team | Pts | Pld | W | D | L | GF | GA |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

| **—** | [Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkey_national_football_team) | *withdrew* |  |  |  |  |  |  |

CAF / AFC second round

Indonesia withdrew after FIFA rejected their request to play against Israel on neutral ground. Israel advanced to the Final Round automatically.

| Rank | Team | Pts | Pld | W | D | L | GF | GA |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

CAF / AFC Final Round

Sudan refused to play against Israel for political reasons, so Israel would technically qualify automatically, but before the qualification rounds began, FIFA ruled that no team would qualify without playing at least one match (except for the defending champions and the hosts), and Israel had yet to play any.

| **—** | [Sudan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sudan_national_football_team) | *withdrew* |  |  |  |  |  |  |

UEFA / AFC Play-off

A special play-off was created between Israel and the runner-up of one of the UEFA Groups, where the teams played against each other on a home-and-away basis, with the winner qualifying. After [Belgium](679716.md) refused, [Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wales_national_football_team), the runner-up of UEFA Group 4, was the team drawn from the UEFA group runners-up.[^cite_note-20]

1962 FIFA World Cup qualifier

At the [1962 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1962_FIFA_World_Cup), Israel competed in the [UEFA Group 7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1962_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_\(UEFA_%E2%80%93_Group_7\)) qualifying round. The round was conducted in a knockout stage format. The five teams in this group played in a knockout stage on a home-and-away basis, with Israel finishing in second place, after losing to Italy in the finals.[^cite_note-21]

1966 FIFA World Cup qualifier

At the [1966 FIFA World Cup](61629.md), Israel competed in the [UEFA Group 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1966_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_\(UEFA_%E2%80%93_Group_1\)) qualifying round, finishing in third and last place.[^cite_note-22] Israeli referee [Menachem Ashkenazi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menachem_Ashkenazi) also participated in the World Cup, officiating the [Group 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1966_FIFA_World_Cup_Group_1) game between France and Mexico, as well as the [Quarter-finals](61629.md#Quarter-finals) game between Portugal and North Korea.[^cite_note-23]

1974 FIFA World Cup qualifier

At the [1974 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_FIFA_World_Cup), Israel competed in the [AFC and OFC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_\(AFC_and_OFC\)) qualifying round, losing in the [Zone A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_AFC_and_OFC_Zone_A) finals.[^cite_note-1974rsssf-24] The 1974 World Cup was Israel's last as an official member of the AFC, as they resigned the [Asian Games Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Games_Federation) in 1974.[^cite_note-1978uefa-25]

Classification matches

Based on the results of the classification match Israel was assigned to Group 2 with Thailand and Malaysia.

Semi-finals

Israel, defeating Japan in the semi-finals, advanced to the Zone A finals to face South Korea.

Finals

In the Zone A finals, Israel lost to South Korea, who moved on to the AFC/OFC [Final round](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_\(AFC_and_OFC\)#Final_round).

1978 FIFA World Cup qualifier

At the [1978 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1978_FIFA_World_Cup), Israel competed in the [AFC and OFC Group 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1978_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_AFC_and_OFC_first_round?action=edit\&redlink=1#Group_2) qualifying round, finishing in second place.[^cite_note-1978rsssf-26] Israel, despite resigning the [Asian Games Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Games_Federation) in 1974, was still assigned to compete in the AFC and OFC qualifying round.[^cite_note-1978uefa-25]

Additionally Israeli referee, [Abraham Klein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham_Klein_\(referee\)), worked games in [Group 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1978_FIFA_World_Cup_Group_1), [Group A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austria_v_West_Germany_\(1978_FIFA_World_Cup\)) and the [match for third place](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1978_FIFA_World_Cup#Match_for_third_place).[^cite_note-27]

1982 FIFA World Cup qualifier

At the [1982 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982_FIFA_World_Cup), Israel competed in the [UEFA Group 6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_UEFA_Group_6) qualifying round, finishing in fifth and last place.[^cite_note-rsssf-29]

Israel was previously part of the [Asian Games Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Games_Federation) until it was disbanded in 1981. In 1982 Israel was barred joining the new [Olympic Council of Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympic_Council_of_Asia), Israel opted to join the [European Olympic Committees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Olympic_Committees) in the early 1990s. The 1982 World Cup Israel was not yet part of the continent group, however was assigned to it.[^cite_note-unassigned-30]

1986 FIFA World Cup qualifier

At the [1986 FIFA World Cup](157231.md), [Israel](9282173.md) competed in the [OFC qualification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1986_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_\(OFC\)) round, finishing in second place.

Israel was previously part of the [Asian Games Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Games_Federation) until it was disbanded in 1981. In 1982 Israel was barred joining the new [Olympic Council of Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympic_Council_of_Asia), Israel opted to join the [European Olympic Committees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Olympic_Committees) in the early 1990s. The 1986 World Cup Israel was not yet part of a continent group, therefore joining OFC.[^cite_note-unassigned-30]

1990 FIFA World Cup qualifier

At the [1990 FIFA World Cup](157232.md), [Israel](9282173.md) competed in the [OFC qualification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1990_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_\(OFC\)) round, finishing in first place. Israel went on to face [Colombia](680175.md) in the [CONMBOL vs. OFC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1990_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_\(CONMEBOL%E2%80%93OFC_play-off\)) playoff, losing 1–0.

Israel was previously part of the [Asian Games Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Games_Federation) until it was disbanded in 1981. In 1982 Israel was barred joining the new [Olympic Council of Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympic_Council_of_Asia), Israel opted to join the [European Olympic Committees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Olympic_Committees) in the early 1990s. The 1990 World Cup Israel was not yet part of a continent group, therefore joining OFC.[^cite_note-unassigned-30]

OFC qualification&#x20;

Round 1

In [round 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1990_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_OFC_first_round), [Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_national_football_team) received a bye and advanced to the final round directly. The remaining four teams were paired up to play knockout matches on a home-and-away basis. The winners would advance to the Second Round.

Round 2

In [round 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1990_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_OFC_second_round) Israel competed against Australia and New Zealand, finishing in first place.&#x20;

CONMEBOL vs OFC playoff

Israel, as the winning team of the OFC qualification tournament played the [CONMEBOL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1990_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_\(CONMEBOL\)) group winner with the weakest record in a home-and-away play-off. The winner of this play-off qualified for the 1990 FIFA World Cup.

1994 FIFA World Cup qualifier

At the [1994 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_FIFA_World_Cup), Israel competed in the [UEFA Group 6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_\(UEFA_%E2%80%93_Group_6\)) qualifying round, finishing in sixth and last place.[^cite_note-32] Israel was previously part of the [Asian Games Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Games_Federation) until it was disbanded in 1981. In 1982 Israel was barred joining the new [Olympic Council of Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympic_Council_of_Asia), Israel opted to join the [European Olympic Committees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Olympic_Committees) in the early 1990s. The 1994 World Cup was the first in which Israel competed in Europe.[^cite_note-unassigned-30]

2002 FIFA World Cup qualifier

At the [2002 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_FIFA_World_Cup), Israel competed in the [UEFA Group 7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_UEFA_Group_7) qualifying round, finishing in third place.[^cite_note-34]

2006 FIFA World Cup qualifier

At the [2006 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_FIFA_World_Cup), Israel competed in the [UEFA Group 4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_UEFA_Group_4) qualifying round, finishing in third place.

2010 FIFA World Cup qualifier

At the [2010 FIFA World Cup](168079.md), Israel competed in the [UEFA Group 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_UEFA_Group_2) qualifying round, finishing in fourth place.

2014 FIFA World Cup qualifier

At the [2014 FIFA World Cup](656933.md), Israel competed in the [UEFA Group F](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_UEFA_Group_F) qualifying round, finishing in third place.

2018 FIFA World Cup qualifier

At the [2018 FIFA World Cup](13327177.md), Israel competed in the [UEFA Group G](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_UEFA_Group_G) qualifying round, finishing in fourth place.

2022 FIFA World Cup qualifier

At the [2022 FIFA World Cup](17742072.md), Israel competed in the [UEFA Group F](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_UEFA_Group_F) qualifying round, finishing in third place.

2026 FIFA World Cup qualifier

At the [2026 FIFA World Cup](36581929.md), Israel competed in the [UEFA Group I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_UEFA_Group_I) qualifying round, finishing in third place.

## Record players

Ten players were fielded in all three of Israel's group matches in 1970 by coach [Emmanuel Scheffer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emmanuel_Scheffer), making them record World Cup players for their country.

| Rank | Player | Matches |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | [Shraga Bar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shraga_Bar) | 3 |
| [Yehoshua Feigenbaum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yehoshua_Feigenbaum) | 3 |  |
| [David Primo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Primo) | 3 |  |
| [Zvi Rosen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zvi_Rosen) | 3 |  |
| [Shmuel Rosenthal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shmuel_Rosenthal) | 3 |  |
| [Yisha'ayahu Schwager](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yisha'ayahu_Schwager) | 3 |  |
| [Itzhak Shum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Itzhak_Shum) | 3 |  |
| [Giora Spiegel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giora_Spiegel) | 3 |  |
| [Mordechai Spiegler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mordechai_Spiegler) | 3 |  |
| [Itzhak Vissoker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Itzhak_Vissoker) | 3 |  |

## Goalscorers

Israel's sole international goal at a FIFA World Cup final-tournament was scored by team captain [Mordechai Spiegler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mordechai_Spiegler) in their 1–1 draw against [Sweden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sweden_men's_national_football_team) on 7 June 1970.
